+"""Process raw fire and climate data into a unified tabular dataset.
+
+Steps:
+1. Load fire hotspot data, assign each hotspot to a grid cell and date.
+2. Aggregate: count hotspots per cell per day.
+3. Load and merge climate data for each cell.
+4. Create the binary target: at least one hotspot in the cell in the next 7 days.
+5. Save the processed dataset.
+
+IMPORTANT: The target is computed BEFORE feature engineering to keep the logic
+clear. The target for day D uses fire data from days D+1 to D+7, which is
+information from the future that the model must predict, NOT use as input.
+"""
+
+import pandas as pd
+import numpy as np
+from pathlib import Path
+
+from src.config import get_project_root, load_config
+from src.data.build_grid import build_grid, assign_to_grid
+
+
+def process_data(config: dict | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
+    """Build the processed dataset from raw fire + climate data.
+
+    Args:
+        config: Optional config dict.
+
+    Returns:
+        Processed DataFrame saved to data/processed/dataset.parquet.
+    """
+    cfg = config or load_config()
+    root = get_project_root()
+    horizon = cfg["model"]["forecast_horizon_days"]
+
+    # --- 1. Load and assign fire data to grid ---
+    print("Step 1: Loading fire data...")
+    fire_dir = root / "data" / "raw" / "fire"
+    state = cfg["region"]["state_code"]
+    fire_file = fire_dir / f"focos_{state}_all.csv"
+    if not fire_file.exists():
+        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Combined fire file not found: {fire_file}")
+
+    fire_df = pd.read_csv(fire_file)
+
+    # Parse date from the datetime column
+    if "data_hora_gmt" in fire_df.columns:
+        fire_df["date"] = pd.to_datetime(
+            fire_df["data_hora_gmt"], errors="coerce"
+        ).dt.date
+    elif "datahora" in fire_df.columns:
+        fire_df["date"] = pd.to_datetime(
+            fire_df["datahora"], errors="coerce"
+        ).dt.date
+    else:
+        # Try common alternatives
+        date_cols = [c for c in fire_df.columns if "data" in c.lower() or "date" in c.lower()]
+        if date_cols:
+            fire_df["date"] = pd.to_datetime(fire_df[date_cols[0]], errors="coerce").dt.date
+        else:
+            raise ValueError(f"No date column found. Columns: {list(fire_df.columns)}")
+
+    fire_df = fire_df.dropna(subset=["date", "lat", "lon"])
+    fire_df["date"] = pd.to_datetime(fire_df["date"])
+
+    # Assign to grid cells
+    grid_row, grid_col = assign_to_grid(fire_df["lat"].values, fire_df["lon"].values, cfg)
+    fire_df["grid_row"] = grid_row
+    fire_df["grid_col"] = grid_col
+    fire_df["grid_id"] = fire_df["grid_row"].apply(lambda x: f"{x:03d}") + "_" + fire_df["grid_col"].apply(lambda x: f"{x:03d}")
+
+    # Aggregate: count hotspots per cell per day
+    fire_daily = (
+        fire_df.groupby(["grid_id", "date"])
+        .agg(fire_count=("lat", "size"))
+        .reset_index()
+    )
+    print(f"  Fire data: {len(fire_df):,} hotspots -> {len(fire_daily):,} cell-day records")
+
+    # --- 2. Build full date × cell grid ---
+    print("Step 2: Building full date × cell grid...")
+    grid_df = build_grid(cfg)
+    date_range = pd.date_range(
+        start=f"{cfg['data']['start_year']}-01-01",
+        end=f"{cfg['data']['end_year']}-12-31",
+        freq="D",
+    )
+
+    # Create all cell-date combinations
+    full_index = pd.MultiIndex.from_product(
+        [grid_df["grid_id"].values, date_range],
+        names=["grid_id", "date"],
+    )
+    dataset = pd.DataFrame(index=full_index).reset_index()
+    print(f"  Full grid: {len(dataset):,} cell-day records "
+          f"({len(grid_df)} cells × {len(date_range)} days)")
+
+    # Merge fire counts
+    dataset = dataset.merge(fire_daily, on=["grid_id", "date"], how="left")
+    dataset["fire_count"] = dataset["fire_count"].fillna(0).astype(int)
+
+    # --- 3. Create binary target ---
+    print(f"Step 3: Creating binary target (horizon={horizon} days)...")
+    # For each cell, the target at day D is: any fire in [D+1, D+7]?
+    # We compute a rolling sum over the NEXT 7 days.
+    # Sorting by date ascending, a forward-looking window = shift(-1) then rolling(7).
+    dataset = dataset.sort_values(["grid_id", "date"]).reset_index(drop=True)
+
+    # Use groupby + shift to look into the future
+    def _compute_target(group: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
+        # Sum of fire_count in the next `horizon` days (exclusive of current day)
+        future_fires = (
+            group["fire_count"]
+            .shift(-1)                       # start from tomorrow
+            .rolling(window=horizon, min_periods=1)  # next 7 days
+            .sum()
+            .shift(-(horizon - 1))           # align to the first future day
+        )
+        return (future_fires > 0).astype(int)
+
+    dataset["target"] = (
+        dataset.groupby("grid_id", group_keys=False)
+        .apply(_compute_target)
+    )
+
+    # Drop rows where target cannot be computed (last 7 days of the dataset)
+    dataset = dataset.dropna(subset=["target"])
+    dataset["target"] = dataset["target"].astype(int)
+
+    # --- 4. Merge climate data ---
+    print("Step 4: Merging climate data...")
+    climate_dir = root / "data" / "raw" / "climate"
+    climate_frames: list[pd.DataFrame] = []
+    for csv_file in sorted(climate_dir.glob("climate_*.csv")):
+        cdf = pd.read_csv(csv_file)
+        cdf["date"] = pd.to_datetime(cdf["date"])
+        climate_frames.append(cdf)
+
+    if climate_frames:
+        climate_df = pd.concat(climate_frames, ignore_index=True)
+        dataset = dataset.merge(climate_df, on=["grid_id", "date"], how="left")
+        print(f"  Climate data merged: {len(climate_df):,} records")
+    else:
+        print("  [warn] No climate data found. Proceeding without climate variables.")
+
+    # --- 5. Add grid metadata ---
+    print("Step 5: Adding grid metadata...")
+    grid_meta = grid_df[["grid_id", "grid_row", "grid_col", "lat_center", "lon_center"]]
+    dataset = dataset.merge(grid_meta, on="grid_id", how="left")
+
+    # --- 6. Save ---
+    out_dir = root / "data" / "processed"
+    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    out_path = out_dir / "dataset.parquet"
+    dataset.to_parquet(out_path, index=False)
+    print(f"\nDataset saved: {out_path}")
+    print(f"  Shape: {dataset.shape}")
+    print(f"  Target distribution: {dataset['target'].value_counts().to_dict()}")
+    print(f"  Date range: {dataset['date'].min()} to {dataset['date'].max()}")
+
+    return dataset
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    print("=" * 60)
+    print("Processing raw data into tabular dataset")
+    print("=" * 60)
+    process_data()
+    print("\nDone!")

diff --git a/src/features/build_features.py b/src/features/build_features.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..5e3e7dd
--- /dev/null
+++ b/src/features/build_features.py
@@ -0,0 +1,199 @@
+"""Feature engineering for wildfire risk prediction.
+
+All features use ONLY information available up to day D (the prediction date).
+No future data leaks into features. This is enforced by:
+1. All rolling windows look BACKWARD in time.
+2. Fire count on day D itself IS available (the satellite has already passed).
+3. The target uses days D+1 to D+7 (strictly future).
+
+Features created:
+- Fire history: count of hotspots in the cell over 7/14/30 days, and same
+  period last year. Also neighbor cell fire counts.
+- Climate: rolling means/sums for temperature, humidity, precipitation, wind.
+- Dry spell: consecutive days without rain (precip < 1mm).
+- Seasonality: cyclic encoding of month and day-of-year using sin/cos.
+- Position: latitude and longitude of the cell center.
+"""
+
+import numpy as np
+import pandas as pd
+
+from src.config import get_project_root, load_config
+
+
+def build_features(config: dict | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
+    """Load the processed dataset and add engineered features.
+
+    Args:
+        config: Optional config dict.
+
+    Returns:
+        DataFrame with all features added, saved to data/processed/features.parquet.
+    """
+    cfg = config or load_config()
+    root = get_project_root()
+
+    # Load processed dataset
+    ds_path = root / "data" / "processed" / "dataset.parquet"
+    df = pd.read_parquet(ds_path)
+    df = df.sort_values(["grid_id", "date"]).reset_index(drop=True)
+
+    print(f"Loaded dataset: {df.shape}")
+    print("Building features (backward-looking only)...")
+
+    # --- 1. Fire history features (per cell) ---
+    print("  [1/5] Fire history...")
+    for window in [7, 14, 30]:
+        df[f"fire_count_{window}d"] = (
+            df.groupby("grid_id")["fire_count"]
+            .transform(lambda s: s.rolling(window, min_periods=1).sum())
+        )
+
+    # Same period last year (365 days ago ± 7 days window)
+    df["fire_count_lastyear"] = (
+        df.groupby("grid_id")["fire_count"]
+        .transform(lambda s: s.shift(358).rolling(14, min_periods=1).sum())
+    )
+
+    # --- 2. Neighbor fire counts ---
+    print("  [2/5] Neighbor fire activity...")
+    # Pivot to get fire_count per day per cell, then for each cell sum neighbors
+    _add_neighbor_features(df, cfg)
+
+    # --- 3. Climate rolling features ---
+    print("  [3/5] Climate features...")
+    climate_vars = ["T2M_MAX", "T2M_MIN", "RH2M", "PRECTOTCORR", "WS10M"]
+    existing_climate = [v for v in climate_vars if v in df.columns]
+
+    for var in existing_climate:
+        for window in [7, 14]:
+            df[f"{var}_mean_{window}d"] = (
+                df.groupby("grid_id")[var]
+                .transform(lambda s: s.rolling(window, min_periods=1).mean())
+            )
+        if var == "PRECTOTCORR":
+            # Accumulated precipitation
+            for window in [7, 14, 30]:
+                df[f"precip_sum_{window}d"] = (
+                    df.groupby("grid_id")[var]
+                    .transform(lambda s: s.rolling(window, min_periods=1).sum())
+                )
+
+    # --- 4. Dry spell (consecutive days without rain) ---
+    print("  [4/5] Dry spell...")
+    if "PRECTOTCORR" in df.columns:
+        df["is_dry"] = (df["PRECTOTCORR"].fillna(0) < 1.0).astype(int)
+        df["dry_days"] = (
+            df.groupby("grid_id")["is_dry"]
+            .transform(_consecutive_count)
+        )
+        df = df.drop(columns=["is_dry"])
+    else:
+        df["dry_days"] = 0
+
+    # --- 5. Seasonality and position ---
+    print("  [5/5] Seasonality and position...")
+    df["month"] = df["date"].dt.month
+    df["day_of_year"] = df["date"].dt.dayofyear
+
+    # Cyclic encoding: sin/cos transform preserves continuity
+    # (Dec 31 is close to Jan 1, unlike raw month numbers)
+    df["month_sin"] = np.sin(2 * np.pi * df["month"] / 12)
+    df["month_cos"] = np.cos(2 * np.pi * df["month"] / 12)
+    df["doy_sin"] = np.sin(2 * np.pi * df["day_of_year"] / 365)
+    df["doy_cos"] = np.cos(2 * np.pi * df["day_of_year"] / 365)
+
+    # Position features (already in the dataset as lat_center, lon_center)
+    # These help the model learn spatial patterns without explicit spatial modeling
+
+    # --- Drop intermediate columns ---
+    cols_to_drop = ["fire_count", "month", "day_of_year"]
+    cols_to_drop = [c for c in cols_to_drop if c in df.columns]
+    df = df.drop(columns=cols_to_drop)
+
+    # --- Save ---
+    out_path = root / "data" / "processed" / "features.parquet"
+    df.to_parquet(out_path, index=False)
+
+    feature_cols = [c for c in df.columns if c not in ("grid_id", "date", "target", "grid_row", "grid_col")]
+    print(f"\nFeatures saved: {out_path}")
+    print(f"  Shape: {df.shape}")
+    print(f"  Feature count: {len(feature_cols)}")
+    print(f"  Features: {feature_cols}")
+
+    return df
+
+
+def _consecutive_count(series: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
+    """Count consecutive True/1 values ending at each position.
+
+    For example, [1, 1, 0, 1, 1, 1] → [1, 2, 0, 1, 2, 3].
+    """
+    groups = (series != series.shift()).cumsum()
+    return series.groupby(groups).cumsum()
+
+
+def _add_neighbor_features(df: pd.DataFrame, cfg: dict) -> None:
+    """Add fire counts from neighboring grid cells (in-place).
+
+    For each cell, sums fire_count_7d from the 8 surrounding cells.
+    This captures spatial fire spread patterns.
+    """
+    # Build a lookup of (row, col) neighbors
+    if "grid_row" not in df.columns or "grid_col" not in df.columns:
+        df["neighbor_fire_7d"] = 0
+        return
+
+    # Pivot: date × grid_id → fire_count_7d
+    fire_7d_pivot = df.pivot_table(
+        index="date", columns="grid_id", values="fire_count_7d", fill_value=0
+    )
+
+    # For each cell, identify its 8 neighbors and sum
+    grid_lookup = df[["grid_id", "grid_row", "grid_col"]].drop_duplicates()
+    neighbor_map: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
+
+    for _, row in grid_lookup.iterrows():
+        r, c = int(row["grid_row"]), int(row["grid_col"])
+        neighbors = []
+        for dr in [-1, 0, 1]:
+            for dc in [-1, 0, 1]:
+                if dr == 0 and dc == 0:
+                    continue
+                nid = f"{r + dr:03d}_{c + dc:03d}"
+                if nid in fire_7d_pivot.columns:
+                    neighbors.append(nid)
+        neighbor_map[row["grid_id"]] = neighbors
+
+    # Compute neighbor sums
+    neighbor_sums = {}
+    for gid, neighbors in neighbor_map.items():
+        if neighbors:
+            neighbor_sums[gid] = fire_7d_pivot[neighbors].sum(axis=1)
+        else:
+            neighbor_sums[gid] = pd.Series(0, index=fire_7d_pivot.index)
+
+    # Melt back and merge
+    neighbor_df = pd.DataFrame(neighbor_sums)
+    neighbor_df.index.name = "date"
+    neighbor_melted = neighbor_df.reset_index().melt(
+        id_vars="date", var_name="grid_id", value_name="neighbor_fire_7d"
+    )
+    neighbor_melted["date"] = pd.to_datetime(neighbor_melted["date"])
+
+    # Merge into main dataframe
+    df_idx = df.set_index(["grid_id", "date"]).index
+    neighbor_melted = neighbor_melted.set_index(["grid_id", "date"])
+    df["neighbor_fire_7d"] = df.set_index(["grid_id", "date"]).join(
+        neighbor_melted, how="left"
+    )["neighbor_fire_7d"].values
+    df["neighbor_fire_7d"] = df["neighbor_fire_7d"].fillna(0)
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    print("=" * 60)
+    print("Building features")
+    print("=" * 60)
+    build_features()
+    print("\nDone!")
+
diff --git a/src/models/__init__.py b/src/models/__init__.py
new file mode 100644

+"""Model explainability with SHAP.
+
+Generates SHAP summary plots and feature importance analysis.
+Uses TreeExplainer for LightGBM (exact, fast computation).
+"""
+
+import joblib
+import numpy as np
+import pandas as pd
+import shap
+
+import matplotlib
+matplotlib.use("Agg")
+import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
+
+from src.config import get_project_root, load_config
+
+
+def explain_model(config: dict | None = None) -> None:
+    """Generate SHAP explanations for the LightGBM model."""
+    cfg = config or load_config()
+    root = get_project_root()
+    figures_dir = root / "reports" / "figures"
+    figures_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+
+    # Load model and data
+    model = joblib.load(root / "models" / "lightgbm_model.joblib")
+    feature_cols = joblib.load(root / "models" / "feature_cols.joblib")
+
+    df = pd.read_parquet(root / "data" / "processed" / "features.parquet")
+    df["date"] = pd.to_datetime(df["date"])
+
+    test_mask = df["date"].dt.year >= cfg["model"]["test_start_year"]
+    test_df = df[test_mask].copy()
+
+    X_test = test_df[feature_cols].values.astype(np.float32)
+    X_test = np.nan_to_num(X_test, nan=0.0)
+
+    # Sample for SHAP (full test set can be too large)
+    n_sample = min(5000, len(X_test))
+    rng = np.random.RandomState(cfg["model"]["seed"])
+    idx = rng.choice(len(X_test), n_sample, replace=False)
+    X_sample = X_test[idx]
+
+    # SHAP TreeExplainer (exact for tree models, no approximation needed)
+    print("Computing SHAP values...")
+    explainer = shap.TreeExplainer(model)
+    shap_values = explainer.shap_values(X_sample)
+
+    # For binary classification, shap_values may be a list [class_0, class_1]
+    if isinstance(shap_values, list):
+        shap_vals = shap_values[1]  # class 1 = fire
+    else:
+        shap_vals = shap_values
+
+    # ── Summary plot (beeswarm) ───────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("Generating SHAP summary plot...")
+    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 8))
+    shap.summary_plot(
+        shap_vals,
+        X_sample,
+        feature_names=feature_cols,
+        show=False,
+        max_display=20,
+    )
+    plt.tight_layout()
+    plt.savefig(figures_dir / "shap_summary.png", dpi=150, bbox_inches="tight")
+    plt.close("all")
+    print("  Saved shap_summary.png")
+
+    # ── Bar plot (mean absolute SHAP) ─────────────────────────────────────
+    print("Generating SHAP bar plot...")
+    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 8))
+    shap.summary_plot(
+        shap_vals,
+        X_sample,
+        feature_names=feature_cols,
+        plot_type="bar",
+        show=False,
+        max_display=20,
+    )
+    plt.tight_layout()
+    plt.savefig(figures_dir / "shap_bar.png", dpi=150, bbox_inches="tight")
+    plt.close("all")
+    print("  Saved shap_bar.png")
+
+    # ── Feature importance table ──────────────────────────────────────────
+    mean_shap = np.abs(shap_vals).mean(axis=0)
+    importance_df = pd.DataFrame({
+        "feature": feature_cols,
+        "mean_abs_shap": mean_shap,
+    }).sort_values("mean_abs_shap", ascending=False)
+
+    importance_df.to_csv(root / "reports" / "feature_importance.csv", index=False)
+    print("\nTop 15 features by mean |SHAP|:")
+    print(importance_df.head(15).to_string(index=False))
+
+    # ── Plain-language interpretation ─────────────────────────────────────
+    print("\n" + "=" * 50)
+    print("INTERPRETATION (plain language)")
+    print("=" * 50)
+    top5 = importance_df.head(5)["feature"].tolist()
+    interpretations = {
+        "fire_count_7d": "Recent fire activity in the cell (last 7 days) is the strongest predictor — fires tend to persist and recur in the same area.",
+        "fire_count_14d": "Two-week fire history captures longer burning periods and areas with sustained activity.",
+        "fire_count_30d": "Monthly fire accumulation shows areas in active burning seasons.",
+        "neighbor_fire_7d": "Fire in surrounding cells indicates spatial spread risk — fires don't respect grid boundaries.",
+        "fire_count_lastyear": "Same period last year captures annual patterns — areas that burned before are likely to burn again.",
+        "dry_days": "Consecutive days without rain is a key risk factor — dry vegetation is more flammable.",
+        "precip_sum_7d": "Weekly precipitation (inversely related to risk) — rain reduces fire danger.",
+        "precip_sum_14d": "Two-week precipitation accumulation indicates soil moisture levels.",
+        "precip_sum_30d": "Monthly precipitation captures longer drought patterns.",
+        "RH2M_mean_7d": "Low humidity dries out vegetation, increasing fire risk.",
+        "T2M_MAX_mean_7d": "Higher temperatures accelerate drying and increase fire risk.",
+        "WS10M_mean_7d": "Wind speed affects fire spread rate and intensity.",
+        "doy_sin": "Day-of-year (sine component) captures the fire season cycle — peak around August-October.",
+        "doy_cos": "Day-of-year (cosine component) complements the sine to encode full annual cycle.",
+        "month_sin": "Monthly seasonality — fire season peaks in the dry months.",
+        "month_cos": "Monthly seasonality (cosine component).",
+        "lat_center": "Latitude captures north-south climate gradients across Mato Grosso.",
+        "lon_center": "Longitude captures east-west differences (Cerrado vs. Amazon).",
+    }
+
+    for feat in top5:
+        desc = interpretations.get(feat, "This feature captures patterns the model learned from the data.")
+        print(f"  • {feat}: {desc}")
+
+    print("\nExplainability complete!")
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    print("=" * 60)
+    print("Model Explainability with SHAP")
+    print("=" * 60)
+    explain_model()
+
diff --git a/src/models/train.py b/src/models/train.py
new file mode 100644

+++ b/src/models/train.py
@@ -0,0 +1,398 @@
+"""Train baseline and ML models for wildfire risk prediction.
+
+Models:
+1. Persistence baseline: predicts 1 if there was a fire in the cell recently.
+2. Climatology baseline: predicts the historical frequency for that cell/month.
+3. Logistic Regression: linear reference model.
+4. LightGBM: gradient boosting with Optuna hyperparameter tuning.
+
+Validation strategy:
+- Temporal split: train on years <= 2023, test on 2024-2025.
+- Spatial cross-validation: GroupKFold by grid_id to test generalization.
+
+The class imbalance is handled with scale_pos_weight (LightGBM) and
+class_weight='balanced' (LogReg), which upweight the minority class without
+discarding data — important for time series where every observation matters.
+"""
+
+import json
+import warnings
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import joblib
+import lightgbm as lgb
+import numpy as np
+import optuna
+import pandas as pd
+from sklearn.calibration import CalibratedClassifierCV, calibration_curve
+from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression
+from sklearn.metrics import (
+    average_precision_score,
+    brier_score_loss,
+    precision_recall_curve,
+    roc_auc_score,
+)
+from sklearn.model_selection import GroupKFold
+from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler
+
+import matplotlib
+matplotlib.use("Agg")
+import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
+
+from src.config import get_project_root, load_config
+
+warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning)
+optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)
+
+
+# ── Feature columns ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+FEATURE_COLS: list[str] = []   # populated dynamically
+META_COLS = {"grid_id", "date", "target", "grid_row", "grid_col"}
+
+
+def _get_feature_cols(df: pd.DataFrame) -> list[str]:
+    """Return feature column names (everything except metadata and target)."""
+    return sorted([c for c in df.columns if c not in META_COLS])
+
+
+# ── Baselines ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+def persistence_baseline(df: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:
+    """Persistence baseline: probability = recent fire activity.
+
+    Uses fire_count_7d normalized to [0, 1] as a rough probability.
+    If fire_count_7d > 0, predicts higher risk.
+    """
+    if "fire_count_7d" in df.columns:
+        raw = df["fire_count_7d"].fillna(0).values
+        # Sigmoid-like normalization
+        return 1 - np.exp(-raw / 3.0)
+    return np.zeros(len(df))
+
+
+def climatology_baseline(
+    train_df: pd.DataFrame, test_df: pd.DataFrame
+) -> np.ndarray:
+    """Climatology baseline: historical fire frequency by cell and month.
+
+    For each (grid_id, month), compute the fraction of days with target=1
+    in the training set. Apply these rates to the test set.
+    """
+    train_copy = train_df.copy()
+    train_copy["month"] = train_copy["date"].dt.month
+    rates = train_copy.groupby(["grid_id", "month"])["target"].mean().reset_index()
+    rates.columns = ["grid_id", "month", "clim_prob"]
+
+    test_copy = test_df[["grid_id", "date"]].copy()
+    test_copy["month"] = test_copy["date"].dt.month
+    test_copy = test_copy.merge(rates, on=["grid_id", "month"], how="left")
+    return test_copy["clim_prob"].fillna(0).values
+
+
+# ── Metrics ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+def compute_metrics(
+    y_true: np.ndarray, y_prob: np.ndarray, name: str
+) -> dict:
+    """Compute all evaluation metrics.
+
+    Args:
+        y_true: True binary labels.
+        y_prob: Predicted probabilities.
+        name: Model name.
+
+    Returns:
+        Dictionary with model name and metric values.
+    """
+    # Clip probabilities to avoid log(0)
+    y_prob = np.clip(y_prob, 1e-7, 1 - 1e-7)
+
+    pr_auc = average_precision_score(y_true, y_prob)
+    roc_auc = roc_auc_score(y_true, y_prob)
+    brier = brier_score_loss(y_true, y_prob)
+
+    # Recall at precision >= 0.50
+    precision, recall, thresholds = precision_recall_curve(y_true, y_prob)
+    mask = precision >= 0.50
+    recall_at_p50 = recall[mask].max() if mask.any() else 0.0
+
+    return {
+        "model": name,
+        "PR-AUC": round(pr_auc, 4),
+        "ROC-AUC": round(roc_auc, 4),
+        "Brier": round(brier, 4),
+        "Recall@P50": round(recall_at_p50, 4),
+    }
+
+
+# ── Training ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+def train_all(config: dict | None = None) -> None:
+    """Execute the full training pipeline."""
+    cfg = config or load_config()
+    root = get_project_root()
+    seed = cfg["model"]["seed"]
+    np.random.seed(seed)
+
+    # Load features
+    feat_path = root / "data" / "processed" / "features.parquet"
+    df = pd.read_parquet(feat_path)
+    df["date"] = pd.to_datetime(df["date"])
+
+    feature_cols = _get_feature_cols(df)
+    print(f"Dataset: {df.shape}, features: {len(feature_cols)}")
+    print(f"Target distribution:\n{df['target'].value_counts()}\n")
+
+    # ── Temporal split ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    train_end = cfg["model"]["train_end_year"]
+    train_mask = df["date"].dt.year <= train_end
+    test_mask = df["date"].dt.year >= cfg["model"]["test_start_year"]
+
+    train_df = df[train_mask].copy()
+    test_df = df[test_mask].copy()
+    print(f"Train: {len(train_df):,} ({train_df['date'].min().date()} to {train_df['date'].max().date()})")
+    print(f"Test:  {len(test_df):,} ({test_df['date'].min().date()} to {test_df['date'].max().date()})")
+    print(f"Train target: {train_df['target'].value_counts().to_dict()}")
+    print(f"Test target:  {test_df['target'].value_counts().to_dict()}\n")
+
+    X_train = train_df[feature_cols].values.astype(np.float32)
+    y_train = train_df["target"].values
+    X_test = test_df[feature_cols].values.astype(np.float32)
+    y_test = test_df["target"].values
+
+    # Handle NaN
+    X_train = np.nan_to_num(X_train, nan=0.0)
+    X_test = np.nan_to_num(X_test, nan=0.0)
+
+    results: list[dict] = []
+    models_dir = root / "models"
+    models_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    figures_dir = root / "reports" / "figures"
+    figures_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+
+    # ── 1. Persistence baseline ───────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("=" * 50)
+    print("1. Persistence Baseline")
+    print("=" * 50)
+    y_prob_persist = persistence_baseline(test_df)
+    m = compute_metrics(y_test, y_prob_persist, "Persistence")
+    results.append(m)
+    print(f"  {m}\n")
+
+    # ── 2. Climatology baseline ───────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("=" * 50)
+    print("2. Climatology Baseline")
+    print("=" * 50)
+    y_prob_clim = climatology_baseline(train_df, test_df)
+    m = compute_metrics(y_test, y_prob_clim, "Climatology")
+    results.append(m)
+    print(f"  {m}\n")
+
+    # ── 3. Logistic Regression ────────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("=" * 50)
+    print("3. Logistic Regression")
+    print("=" * 50)
+    scaler = StandardScaler()
+    X_train_scaled = scaler.fit_transform(X_train)
+    X_test_scaled = scaler.transform(X_test)
+
+    lr = LogisticRegression(
+        class_weight="balanced",
+        max_iter=1000,
+        random_state=seed,
+        solver="lbfgs",
+    )
+    lr.fit(X_train_scaled, y_train)
+    y_prob_lr = lr.predict_proba(X_test_scaled)[:, 1]
+    m = compute_metrics(y_test, y_prob_lr, "LogisticRegression")
+    results.append(m)
+    print(f"  {m}\n")
+
+    joblib.dump(lr, models_dir / "logistic_regression.joblib")
+    joblib.dump(scaler, models_dir / "scaler.joblib")
+
+    # ── 4. LightGBM with Optuna ───────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("=" * 50)
+    print("4. LightGBM + Optuna")
+    print("=" * 50)
+
+    # Class imbalance ratio for scale_pos_weight
+    neg_count = (y_train == 0).sum()
+    pos_count = (y_train == 1).sum()
+    scale_pos = neg_count / max(pos_count, 1)
+    print(f"  scale_pos_weight: {scale_pos:.2f}")
+
+    # Spatial CV for Optuna (GroupKFold by grid_id)
+    train_groups = train_df["grid_id"].values
+
+    def objective(trial: optuna.Trial) -> float:
+        params = {
+            "objective": "binary",
+            "metric": "average_precision",
+            "verbosity": -1,
+            "seed": seed,
+            "scale_pos_weight": scale_pos,
+            "n_estimators": trial.suggest_int("n_estimators", 100, 500),
+            "learning_rate": trial.suggest_float("learning_rate", 0.01, 0.3, log=True),
+            "max_depth": trial.suggest_int("max_depth", 3, 8),
+            "num_leaves": trial.suggest_int("num_leaves", 15, 63),
+            "min_child_samples": trial.suggest_int("min_child_samples", 20, 200),
+            "subsample": trial.suggest_float("subsample", 0.6, 1.0),
+            "colsample_bytree": trial.suggest_float("colsample_bytree", 0.6, 1.0),
+            "reg_alpha": trial.suggest_float("reg_alpha", 1e-3, 10.0, log=True),
+            "reg_lambda": trial.suggest_float("reg_lambda", 1e-3, 10.0, log=True),
+        }
+
+        gkf = GroupKFold(n_splits=3)
+        scores = []
+        for fold_train_idx, fold_val_idx in gkf.split(X_train, y_train, train_groups):
+            model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(**params)
+            model.fit(
+                X_train[fold_train_idx], y_train[fold_train_idx],
+                eval_set=[(X_train[fold_val_idx], y_train[fold_val_idx])],
+            )
+            preds = model.predict_proba(X_train[fold_val_idx])[:, 1]
+            scores.append(average_precision_score(y_train[fold_val_idx], preds))
+
+        return np.mean(scores)
+
+    n_trials = cfg["model"]["optuna_trials"]
+    study = optuna.create_study(direction="maximize")
+    study.optimize(objective, n_trials=n_trials, show_progress_bar=False)
+
+    print(f"  Best trial: {study.best_trial.number}")
+    print(f"  Best CV PR-AUC: {study.best_value:.4f}")
+    print(f"  Best params: {study.best_params}")
+
+    # Train final model with best params on full training set
+    best_params = {
+        "objective": "binary",
+        "metric": "average_precision",
+        "verbosity": -1,
+        "seed": seed,
+        "scale_pos_weight": scale_pos,
+        **study.best_params,
+    }
+    final_model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(**best_params)
+    final_model.fit(X_train, y_train)
+
+    y_prob_lgb = final_model.predict_proba(X_test)[:, 1]
+    m = compute_metrics(y_test, y_prob_lgb, "LightGBM")
+    results.append(m)
+    print(f"  Test metrics: {m}\n")
+
+    # Save model
+    joblib.dump(final_model, models_dir / "lightgbm_model.joblib")
+    joblib.dump(feature_cols, models_dir / "feature_cols.joblib")
+
+    # ── 5. Calibration ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("=" * 50)
+    print("5. Calibration Check")
+    print("=" * 50)
+
+    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 1, figsize=(8, 6))
+    for name, probs in [("LogReg", y_prob_lr), ("LightGBM", y_prob_lgb), ("Climatology", y_prob_clim)]:
+        prob_true, prob_pred = calibration_curve(y_test, probs, n_bins=10, strategy="uniform")
+        ax.plot(prob_pred, prob_true, marker="o", label=name)
+    ax.plot([0, 1], [0, 1], "k--", label="Perfect")
+    ax.set_xlabel("Mean predicted probability")
+    ax.set_ylabel("Fraction of positives")
+    ax.set_title("Calibration Curve")
+    ax.legend()
+    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
+    fig.tight_layout()
+    fig.savefig(figures_dir / "calibration_curve.png", dpi=150)
+    plt.close()
+    print("  Saved calibration_curve.png")
+
+    # Calibrate LightGBM if needed (Platt scaling)
+    cal_model = CalibratedClassifierCV(final_model, cv=3, method="isotonic")
+    cal_model.fit(X_train, y_train)
+    y_prob_cal = cal_model.predict_proba(X_test)[:, 1]
+    m_cal = compute_metrics(y_test, y_prob_cal, "LightGBM (calibrated)")
+    results.append(m_cal)
+    print(f"  Calibrated metrics: {m_cal}")
+
+    joblib.dump(cal_model, models_dir / "lightgbm_calibrated.joblib")
+
+    # ── 6. Results table ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("\n" + "=" * 50)
+    print("MODEL COMPARISON")
+    print("=" * 50)
+    results_df = pd.DataFrame(results)
+    print(results_df.to_string(index=False))
+    results_df.to_csv(root / "reports" / "model_comparison.csv", index=False)
+
+    # ── 7. Error analysis ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    print("\n" + "=" * 50)
+    print("7. Error Analysis")
+    print("=" * 50)
+    _error_analysis(test_df, y_test, y_prob_lgb, figures_dir)
+
+    # ── 8. Save metadata ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    meta = {
+        "feature_cols": feature_cols,
+        "train_period": f"{train_df['date'].min().date()} to {train_df['date'].max().date()}",
+        "test_period": f"{test_df['date'].min().date()} to {test_df['date'].max().date()}",
+        "train_size": len(train_df),
+        "test_size": len(test_df),
+        "best_params": study.best_params,
+        "results": results,
+    }
+    with open(models_dir / "training_metadata.json", "w") as f:
+        json.dump(meta, f, indent=2, default=str)
+
+    print("\nTraining complete!")
+    return results_df
+
+
+def _error_analysis(
+    test_df: pd.DataFrame,
+    y_true: np.ndarray,
+    y_prob: np.ndarray,
+    figures_dir: Path,
+) -> None:
+    """Analyze model errors by month and region."""
+    analysis = test_df[["grid_id", "date", "lat_center", "lon_center"]].copy()
+    analysis["y_true"] = y_true
+    analysis["y_prob"] = y_prob
+    analysis["y_pred"] = (y_prob >= 0.5).astype(int)
+    analysis["correct"] = (analysis["y_true"] == analysis["y_pred"]).astype(int)
+    analysis["month"] = analysis["date"].dt.month
+
+    # Accuracy by month
+    monthly = analysis.groupby("month").agg(
+        accuracy=("correct", "mean"),
+        n_positive=("y_true", "sum"),
+        total=("y_true", "count"),
+    ).reset_index()
+    print("\n  Performance by month:")
+    print(monthly.to_string(index=False))
+
+    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 5))
+    ax.bar(monthly["month"], monthly["accuracy"], color="steelblue")
+    ax.set_xlabel("Month")
+    ax.set_ylabel("Accuracy")
+    ax.set_title("Model Accuracy by Month")
+    ax.set_xticks(range(1, 13))
+    ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
+    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.3, axis="y")
+    fig.tight_layout()
+    fig.savefig(figures_dir / "accuracy_by_month.png", dpi=150)
+    plt.close()
+
+    # False negatives: where the model misses real fires
+    fn = analysis[(analysis["y_true"] == 1) & (analysis["y_pred"] == 0)]
+    fn_monthly = fn.groupby("month").size().reindex(range(1, 13), fill_value=0)
+    print(f"\n  False negatives by month:\n{fn_monthly.to_string()}")
+
+    print(f"\n  Saved accuracy_by_month.png")
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    print("=" * 60)
+    print("Training wildfire risk models")
+    print("=" * 60)
+    train_all()
+

+# ── Grid tests ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

+class TestGrid:
+    """Tests for grid construction and point assignment."""
+
+    def test_grid_has_required_columns(self):
+        from src.data.build_grid import build_grid
+        cfg = {
+            "grid": {"resolution_deg": 1.0},
+            "region": {"lat_min": -12.0, "lat_max": -10.0, "lon_min": -56.0, "lon_max": -54.0},
+        }
+        grid = build_grid(cfg)
+        required = {"grid_id", "grid_row", "grid_col", "lat_center", "lon_center", "lat_min", "lat_max", "lon_min", "lon_max"}
+        assert required.issubset(set(grid.columns))
+
+    def test_grid_cell_count(self):
+        from src.data.build_grid import build_grid
+        cfg = {
+            "grid": {"resolution_deg": 1.0},
+            "region": {"lat_min": -12.0, "lat_max": -10.0, "lon_min": -56.0, "lon_max": -54.0},
+        }
+        grid = build_grid(cfg)
+        # 2 lat steps × 2 lon steps = 4 cells
+        assert len(grid) == 4
+
+    def test_grid_center_within_bounds(self):
+        from src.data.build_grid import build_grid
+        cfg = {
+            "grid": {"resolution_deg": 0.5},
+            "region": {"lat_min": -12.0, "lat_max": -10.0, "lon_min": -56.0, "lon_max": -54.0},
+        }
+        grid = build_grid(cfg)
+        assert (grid["lat_center"] >= -12.0).all()
+        assert (grid["lat_center"] <= -10.0).all()
+        assert (grid["lon_center"] >= -56.0).all()
+        assert (grid["lon_center"] <= -54.0).all()
+
+    def test_assign_to_grid(self):
+        from src.data.build_grid import assign_to_grid
+        cfg = {
+            "grid": {"resolution_deg": 1.0},
+            "region": {"lat_min": -12.0, "lat_max": -10.0, "lon_min": -56.0, "lon_max": -54.0},
+        }
+        row, col = assign_to_grid(-11.5, -55.5, cfg)
+        assert row == 0
+        assert col == 0
+
+    def test_grid_ids_unique(self):
+        from src.data.build_grid import build_grid
+        cfg = {
+            "grid": {"resolution_deg": 0.5},
+            "region": {"lat_min": -18.0, "lat_max": -7.0, "lon_min": -62.0, "lon_max": -50.0},
+        }
+        grid = build_grid(cfg)
+        assert grid["grid_id"].nunique() == len(grid)

+# ── Feature leak detection test ───────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+class TestLeakDetection:
+    """Tests that feature engineering does not leak future information."""
+
+    def test_no_future_leak_in_fire_features(self):
+        """Verify that fire count features only use past data.
+
+        Strategy: create a synthetic dataset where fires only happen AFTER a
+        certain date. If features at a date BEFORE the fires contain non-zero
+        fire counts, that's a leak.
+        """
+        # Create synthetic data: 2 cells, 60 days
+        dates = pd.date_range("2024-01-01", periods=60, freq="D")
+        grid_ids = ["000_000", "000_001"]
+        rows = []
+        for gid in grid_ids:
+            for d in dates:
+                # Fires only start on day 31 (Feb 1)
+                fire = 5 if d >= pd.Timestamp("2024-02-01") else 0
+                rows.append({
+                    "grid_id": gid,
+                    "date": d,
+                    "fire_count": fire,
+                    "target": 0,  # doesn't matter for this test
+                    "grid_row": int(gid.split("_")[0]),
+                    "grid_col": int(gid.split("_")[1]),
+                    "lat_center": -12.0,
+                    "lon_center": -55.0,
+                    "T2M_MAX": 30.0,
+                    "T2M_MIN": 20.0,
+                    "RH2M": 60.0,
+                    "PRECTOTCORR": 0.0,
+                    "WS10M": 3.0,
+                })
+
+        df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
+
+        # Apply the same rolling logic from build_features
+        for window in [7, 14, 30]:
+            df[f"fire_count_{window}d"] = (
+                df.groupby("grid_id")["fire_count"]
+                .transform(lambda s: s.rolling(window, min_periods=1).sum())
+            )
+
+        # Check: on Jan 31 (day 30), no fire features should be > 0
+        # because fires don't start until Feb 1 (day 31)
+        jan_data = df[df["date"] < pd.Timestamp("2024-02-01")]
+        for window in [7, 14, 30]:
+            col = f"fire_count_{window}d"
+            assert (jan_data[col] == 0).all(), (
+                f"LEAK DETECTED: {col} has non-zero values before fires started! "
+                f"Max value: {jan_data[col].max()}"
+            )
+
+    def test_target_uses_future_data(self):
+        """Verify that the target correctly looks into the future.
+
+        The target at day D should be 1 if there's a fire in [D+1, D+7].
+        """
+        # Simple case: fire only on day 10
+        dates = pd.date_range("2024-01-01", periods=20, freq="D")
+        df = pd.DataFrame({
+            "grid_id": "000_000",
+            "date": dates,
+            "fire_count": [0]*9 + [1] + [0]*10,  # fire on Jan 10
+        })
+
+        # Compute target manually
+        # Days 3-9 should have target=1 (fire on day 10 is within 7-day horizon)
+        # Day 10 itself: target looks at days 11-17, all zero -> target=0
+        # So target=1 for days 3,4,5,6,7,8,9 (indices 2-8)
+        horizon = 7
+        for i in range(len(df)):
+            future_fires = df["fire_count"].iloc[i+1:i+1+horizon].sum()
+            expected = 1 if future_fires > 0 else 0
+            # This is what the target should be
+

+class TestAPI:
+    """Tests for the FastAPI endpoints."""
+
+    @pytest.fixture
+    def client(self):
+        from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
+        from api.main import app

+
+    def test_root_endpoint(self, client):
+        response = client.get("/")
+        assert response.status_code == 200
+        data = response.json()
+        assert "message" in data
+
+    def test_health_endpoint(self, client):
+        response = client.get("/health")
+        assert response.status_code == 200
+        data = response.json()
+        assert "status" in data
+        assert "model_loaded" in data
+
+    def test_predict_endpoint_schema(self, client):
+        """Test that predict endpoint returns proper schema."""
+        response = client.post("/predict", json={
+            "latitude": -12.5,
+            "longitude": -55.0,
+            "date": "2024-08-15",
+        })
+        # May be 503 if model not loaded, but should not be 422 (validation error)
+        assert response.status_code in (200, 503)
+
+    def test_predict_invalid_coordinates(self, client):
+        """Test validation rejects out-of-bounds coordinates."""
+        response = client.post("/predict", json={
+            "latitude": 999.0,
+            "longitude": -55.0,
+            "date": "2024-08-15",
+        })
+        assert response.status_code == 422
+
